4148               	;
4149               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4150               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4151               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4152               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4153               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4154               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4155               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4156               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4157               	; 	'
4158               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4159               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4160               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4161               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4162               	; 	'
4163               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4164               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4165               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4166               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4167               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4168               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4169               	; 	' anybody takes.
4170               	; 	'
4171               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4172               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4173               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4174               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.
4175               	; 	hsp4 = 7			' 1.75 px/frame, every Krook

4200 B8CE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4201 B8D0 0450  20 	b *r0
4202               	;
4203               	; 	' ======================================================================
4204               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4205               	; 	' ======================================================================
4206               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4207               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4208               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4209               	; 	' of running.
4210               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4211               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4212               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4213               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4214               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4215               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4216               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4217               	; 	'
4218               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4219               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4220               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4221               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4222               	; 	'
4223               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4224               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4225               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4226               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4227               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4228               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4229               	; 	'
4230               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4231               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4232               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4233               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4234               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4235               	; 	'
4236               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4237               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4238               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4239               	; draw_screen:
4240               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4241               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4242 B8D2 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8D4 A980     
4243 B8D6 E7F8     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY

     B8DA A980

4341               	;
4342               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4343               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4344               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4345               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4346               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4347               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4348               	; 	'
4349               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4350               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4351               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4352               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4353               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4354               	; 	'
4355               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4356               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4357               	; 	' above it at all.
4358               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4359               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4360               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4361               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4362               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4363               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4364               	; 	'
4365               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4366               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4367               	; beam_one:
4368               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4369               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

4902               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
4903               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
4904               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
4905               	; 			'
4906               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
4907               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
4908               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
4909               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
4910               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
4911               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
4912               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
4913               	; 			'
4914               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
4915               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
4916               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
4917               	; 			'
4918               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
4919               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs
4920               	; 			' past is not what this rule is about.

4998               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
4999               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5000               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5001               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5002               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5003               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.

     BF5E 225A     
5082 BF60 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5083               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5084               	; 			'
5085               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5086               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5087               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5088               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5089               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5090               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5091               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5092               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5093               	; 			'
5094               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5095               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5096               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5097               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5098               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5099               	; 			'
5100               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5101               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5102               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5103               	; 			'
5104               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5105               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5106               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5107               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5108               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5109               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5110               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5111 BF62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF64 21C8     
5112 BF66 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BF68 1F00     
5113 BF6A 0541  14 	inv r1
5114 BF6C 5001  18 	szcb r1,r0
5115 BF6E C040  18 	mov r0,r1

     BF72 21B8

     C666 C704     
5923               cv292
5924               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5925               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5926               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5927               	; 		'
5928               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5929               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5930               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5931               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5932               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5933               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5934               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5935               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5936               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5937               	; 		'
5938               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5939               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5940               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5941               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5942               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5943               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6573               	;
6574               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6575               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6576               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6577               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6578               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6579               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6580               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6581               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6582               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6583               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6584               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6585               	; 	'
6586               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6587               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6588               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6589               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6590               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7149               	;
7150               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7151               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7152               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7153               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7154               	; 	'
7155               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7156               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7157               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7158               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7159               	; 	'
7160               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7161               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7162               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7163               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7164               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7165               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7166               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7167               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7168               	; 	'
7169               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7170               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7171               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7172               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7173               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7174               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7175               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7176               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7177               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7178               	; 	'
7179               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7180               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7181               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7182               	; wipe_shelf:
7183               cvb_WIPE_SHELF

7255               	;
7256               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7257               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7258               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7259               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7260               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7261               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7262               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7263               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7264               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7265               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7266               	; 	' nothing.
7267               	; 	'
7268               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7269               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7270               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7271               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no
7272               	; 	' fixture sits close enough for the two-cell clear at #bca and #bca+1 to
7273               	; 	' reach column 0 or 31.
7274               	; beam_clear:
7275               cvb_BEAM_CLEAR

7583               	; 	' THE SOUND PULSE. Driven off fphs, which already ticks once per pass and

8244               	;
8245               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8246               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8247               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8248               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8249               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8250               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8251               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8252               	; 	'
8253               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8254               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8255               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8256               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8257               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8258               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8259               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8260               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8261               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8262               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8263               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8264               	; 	' flight looking busy.

8808 DBFE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8809 DC00 0450  20 	b *r0
8810               	;
8811               	; 	' ======================================================================
8812               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8813               	; 	' ======================================================================
8814               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8815               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8816               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8817               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8818               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8819               	; 	'
8820               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8821               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8822               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8823               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8824               	; 	' broken.
8825               	; 	'
8826               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8827               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8828               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8829               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8830               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8831               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8832               	; 	'
8833               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8834               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8835               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8836               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8837               	; coll_obst:
8838               cvb_COLL_OBST
8839               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8840 DC02 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DC04 218C     
8841 DC06 0201  20 	li r1,768   ; 3*256
     DC08 0300

     DC10 DC16

9374               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both

9377               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9378               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9379               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9380               	; 	'

9382               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9383               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9384               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

9419               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
9420               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
9421               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
9422               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even
9423               	; 	' when the collision happened off the edge of the player's attention.

9446               cv706
9447               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9448               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9449               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9450               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9451               	; 	'
9452               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9453               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9454               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9455               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9456               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9457               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9458               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
9459               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

9582               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
9583               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
9584               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
9585               	; 		'

10310 E868 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10311 E86A 0450  20 	b *r0
10312               	;
10313               	; 	' ======================================================================
10314               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10315               	; 	' ======================================================================
10316               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10317               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10318               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10319               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10320               	; 	'
10321               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10322               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10323               	; 	'
10324               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10325               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10326               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10327               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10328               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10329               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10330               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10331               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10332               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10333               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10334               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10335               	; esc_tick:
10336               cvb_ESC_TICK
10337               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10338               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10339               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10340               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10341               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10342 E86C D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E86E 217A     
10343 E870 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E872 0100     
10344 E874 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10345 E876 1402  14 	jhe cv778
10346 E878 0460  28 	b @cv777
     E87A E890     
10347               cv778
10348               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10349 E87C D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E87E 217A     
10350 E880 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E882 0700     
10351 E884 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10352 E886 1A02  14 	jl cv780
10353 E888 0460  28 	b @cv779
     E88A E890     
10354               cv780
10355 E88C C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10356 E88E 0450  20 	b *r0
10357               cv779
10358               	; 	END IF
10359               cv777
10360               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10361               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10362               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10363               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10364               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10365               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10366               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10367               	; 	'
10368               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10369               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10370               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10371               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10372               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10373               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10374               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10375               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10376 E890 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E892 20B0     
10377 E894 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E896 0100     
10378 E898 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E89A 20B0     
10379               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
10380 E89C 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     E89E 0400     
10381 E8A0 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10382 E8A2 1402  14 	jhe cv782
10383 E8A4 0460  28 	b @cv781
     E8A6 E8AE     
10384               cv782
10385 E8A8 04C0  14 	clr r0
10386 E8AA D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E8AC 20B0     
10387               cv781
10388               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10389               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10390               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10391               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10392               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10393               	; 	'
10394               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10395               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10396               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10397               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10398               	; 	'
10399               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10400               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10401               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10402               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10403               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10404               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10405               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10406               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
10407               	; 	'
10408               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
10409               	; 	' move at all.
10410               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN

10671               	;
10672               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
10673               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
10674               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
10675               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
10676               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
10677               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
10678               	; 	'
10679               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
10680               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
10681               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
10682               	; 	'
10683               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
10684               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
10685               	; 	'   +2  GREY
10686               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
10687               	; 	'
10688               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
10689               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
10690               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
10691               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
10692               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
10693               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
10694               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
10695               	; 	'
10696               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
10697               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
10698               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
10699               	; 	'
10700               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
10701               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
10702               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
10703               	; scan_furn:
10704               cvb_SCAN_FURN

     EED6 21D1     
11202               	;
11203               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11204               	; 	'
11205               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11206               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11207               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11208               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11209               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11210               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11211               	; 	'
11212               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11213               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11214               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11215               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11216               	; 	'
11217               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11218               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11219               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11220               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11221               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11222               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11223               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11224               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11225               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11226               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11227               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
11228               	; 	say = 3 - elvl
11229 EED8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ELVL,r0
     EEDA 2131     
11230 EEDC 0980  32 	srl r0,8
11231 EEDE C040  18 	mov r0,r1
11232 EEE0 0200  20 	li r0,3
     EEE2 0003

11234 EEE6 0A80  32 	sla r0,8
11235 EEE8 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SAY
     EEEA 21C6

11253               	; 	' the same x leaves their CENTRES two apart -- and an actor standing dead
11254               	; 	' centre in the lift read as standing at its left-hand edge.
11255               	; 	'
11256               	; 	' An actor in the shaft is at store x 120 on screen 3, which is canvas
11257               	; 	' 3*16 + 120/16 = 55, so his three pixels are screen 119-121 and his
11258               	; 	' centre is 120. Five pixels centred on 120 start at 118. The character
11259               	; 	' version had the same two-pixel error (char column 7, bits 248, screen
11260               	; 	' 120-124) and the sprite inherited it.

11932 F4A6 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     F4A8 AA16     
11933               	;
11934               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
11935               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
11936               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
11937               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
11938               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
11939               	; 	'
11940               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
11941               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
11942               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
11943               	; 	'
11944               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
11945               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
11946               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
11947               	; 	'
11948               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
11949               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11950               	; bonus_count:
11951               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
11952               	; 	#bval = 100
11953 F4AA 0200  20 	li r0,100
     F4AC 0064     
11954 F4AE C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__BVAL
     F4B0 20FA     
11955               	; 	IF krk > 9 THEN #bval = 200
11956 F4B2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KRK,r0
     F4B4 20B1     
11957 F4B6 0201  20 	li r1,2560   ; 10*256
     F4B8 0A00

11960 F4BE 0460  28 	b @cv896
     F4C0 F4CA     
11961               cv897
11962 F4C2 0200  20 	li r0,200
     F4C4 00C8     
11963 F4C6 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__BVAL
     F4C8 20FA

     F590 F4E2     
12053               	;
12054               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12055               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12056               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12057               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12058               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12059               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12060               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12061               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12062               	; 	'
12063               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12064               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12065               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12066               	;

12201               	;
12202               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12203               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12204               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12205               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12206               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12207               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12208               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12209               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12210               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12211               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12212               	; 	' Harry was caught.

12313               	; 	' SOUND
12314               	; 	' ======================================================================
12315               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12316               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12317               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12318               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12319               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

12668               	;
12669               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
12670               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
12671               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
12672               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
12673               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12674               	; 	'
12675               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
12676               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
12677               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
12678               	; 	'
12679               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
12680               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
12681               	; 	' letter after it.
12682               	;
12683               	; font_bits:
12684               cvb_FONT_BITS
12685               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
12686 FA98 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     FA9A 0000     
     FA9C 0000     
     FA9E 0000

12803               	;
12804               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
12805               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it. Putting the directive
12806               	; 	' above font.bas would sweep the font in too.
12807               	; 	#if TI994A
12808               	; 	BANK 1
12809      038E     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$

12813 FFFE 6004     	data >6004
12814               	bank 3
12815               	; 	#endif
12816               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12817               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12818               	; 	'
12819               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12820               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12821               	; 	' being small sprites.
12822               	; 	'
12823               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12824               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12825               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12826               	; 	'
12827               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12828               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12829               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12830               	;
12831               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12832               cvb_SPR_KELLY
12833               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00
12834 602A 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     602C 0000     
     602E 0000     
     6030 0000     
12835               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$03,$07,$0F,$0F,$0F,$3F
12836 6032 0000     	byte >00,>00,>03,>07,>0f,>0f,>0f,>3f
     6034 0307     
     6036 0F0F     
     6038 0F3F

13616               	;
13617               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
13618               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
13619               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
13620               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
13621               	;
13622               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
13623               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
13624               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
13625               	; 	' unreadable.
13626               	;
13627               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
13628               cvb_SCAN_COL3

13730               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
13731               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
13732               	; 	'
13733               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
13734               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
13735               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
13736               	; 	'
13737               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
13738               	; 	' blit is
13739               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
13740               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
13741               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
13742               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
13743               	; 	'
13744               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
13745               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
13746               	; 	'
13747               	; 	' template ids:
13748               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
13749               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
13750               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
13751               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
13752               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
13753               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
13754               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
13755               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
13756               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
13757               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
13758               	;
13759               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
13760               cvb_STOR_TPL
